  - **A `.pdf` upload whose first bytes the registry sniffs as no kind passes the spoof check when all of `%PDF-` lies in its first 1,024 bytes.** A file that opens with the signature of a kind the registry compares with the extension (ZIP, PE, ELF, PNG, JPEG, compound file and the rest of its table) is still refused under a `.pdf` name, wherever its `%PDF-` bytes are. A header that starts at byte 1,020 or later (so that it runs past byte 1,024) is refused. The text reader's `%PDF-` check uses the same predicate. A kind the registry sniffs but has no comparison for (a WOFF, TrueType or OpenType font) passes under any name, as it did before this change; that gap is not closed here.
  - **A tool that decides what a file is from its first bytes and does not search for the header is handed the file from `%PDF-` on** (`pdfFromHeader`, one helper beside `hasPdfHeader`: a view of the same memory, and the very same buffer when the header is at byte 0 or outside the window). That is Ghostscript in the compress profiles `web`, `print` and `max` (`compress.ts`) and LibreOffice in the PDF/A conversion (`pdfa.ts`). Poppler's tools and qpdf search the window themselves and are handed the upload. Ghostscript also gets `-P-`, as the `ps2pdf` scripts pass it; the PostScript sources (`postscript-gs.ts`) already use it.
  - **A stream whose /Length is a reference to an object written after it gets the length that object states**, once the whole file is indexed, when `endstream` follows that many bytes with at most 16 bytes of white space between them. Otherwise the stream keeps the end found by searching for `endstream`, as before: the line end in front of the keyword is dropped.
  - **Flate data is inflated without its Adler-32 checksum.** The two-byte zlib header is checked first: deflate method, header check bits, no preset dictionary; a header that fails is a CorruptStreamError (400). Deflate data that is cut short or breaks is still a CorruptStreamError. The per-stream cap (64 MiB) and the document budget (256 MiB) are unchanged and are checked while inflating, so a bomb is refused at the cap whatever its checksum.
  - **A stream with no data bytes reads as empty, whatever its filters.**
  - **A length check, direct or later, looks at most 16 bytes past the data for `endstream`** (`MAX_WHITE_BEFORE_ENDSTREAM`), skipping white space only. A run longer than that is not followed: the length is not used and the stream keeps the end found by searching for `endstream`, with nothing charged. A file whose lengths all end in one long run of white space is read in milliseconds.
- **Why**:
  - **The nightly real-world run of `f44e7b36` refused 85 jobs with these two errors; 61 of them are real PDFs.** 15 jobs over 5 files were `FileExtensionSpoofError` ("initial magic bytes indicate MIME type unknown/corrupted") and 46 jobs over 24 files were `CorruptStreamError`. The other spoof refusals of that run are 21 jobs over 7 Office files (password-protected or fuzzer files named `.docx`, `.xlsx`, `.xls`), and the other CorruptStreamError refusals are 3 jobs of `tika-testhwp_3.0.hwp`. Neither group is PDF; this change does not touch them.
  - **The five spoof refusals are valid PDFs with bytes in front of the header.** `govdocs1-001294.pdf` opens with a CR LF; `-001284`, `-001273`, `-002729` and `-002731` open with a 128-byte MacBinary II header (type `PDF `, creator `CARO`, the classic Mac Acrobat). `qpdf --check` finds no syntax or stream error in any of them, and Poppler reads 11 to 36 pages of text from each. The readers all search the same window, so the check now accepts what they open:

    | Source | Rule | Use here |
    |---|---|---|
    | ISO 32000-1 section 7.5.2, and the implementation note of the PDF Reference 1.7 that Acrobat requires the header only somewhere within the first 1,024 bytes | The header is the first line; readers accept it inside the first kilobyte | The window |
    | pdf.js 6.3.289, `src/core/document.js` lines 943-997 (`PDF_HEADER_SIGNATURE`, `find` with `limit = 1024`) and `checkHeader` (lines 1125-1153, `moveStart` so that offsets count from the header) | The whole signature must lie in the first 1,024 bytes (`scanLength = scanBytes.length - signatureLength`) | The exact bound: a header that starts at byte 1,019 is accepted, one at 1,020 is not |
    | Poppler 25.10, `poppler/PDFDoc.cc` lines 119-121 (`headerSearchSize 1024`) and `PDFDoc::checkHeader` (lines 348-388) | Searches the first 1,024 bytes, warns "May not be a PDF file (continuing anyway)" when there is none | Same window (behaviour only, GPL) |
    | qpdf 12.4.2, `libqpdf/QPDF_objects.cc` lines 224-227 (`findFirst("%PDF-", 0, 1024, ...)`), lines 197-201 (offsets count from the header) | Same window; warns "can't find PDF header" and goes on | Same window |
    | shared-mime-info 2.4, `data/freedesktop.org.xml.in` line 228 (`%PDF-` at offset `0:1024`) | The MIME database that the registry's signatures follow names the same range | The window is the file type's signature, not a parser's leniency |
    | PDFBox 3.0.5, `COSParser.parseHeader` (lines 1617-1698) | Reads lines until one holds `%PDF-` or starts with a digit; with `isLenient` a file without a header only gets a warning | Not followed: it has no byte bound, which the upload gate needs |

  - **21 of the 22 corrupt-stream files were not damaged: the reader cut the failing stream one byte short.** In each of them the stream's /Length is an indirect reference (`/Length 115 0 R` in `govdocs1-004679.pdf`) to an object written after the stream, which the reader's single front-to-back scan had not indexed yet, so it found the end at `endstream` and dropped the line end in front of the keyword. The last data byte of each stream, the last byte of its Adler-32 checksum, is a CR or an LF. In 19 files `endstream` follows it directly, so it was taken for the line end; in `govdocs1-001377.pdf` and `-002166.pdf` a data CR is followed by an LF line end, and the search dropped both. qpdf's raw bytes of the same 21 objects inflate with an unmodified zlib, and its `--filtered-stream-data` matches what the reader now decodes, byte for byte. pdf.js (`src/core/parser.js` line 740, `dict.get("Length")` through the cross-reference table) and PDFBox (`COSParser.parseCOSStream` lines 904-967, `getLength` lines 854-886, `validateStreamLength` lines 1077-1112) resolve the reference before any search. The check that `endstream` follows the length is PDFBox's `validateStreamLength`; the fallback search and its line-end rule are PDFBox's `readUntilEndStream` (lines 983-1075, "drops trailing CR LF and LF").
  - **`govdocs1-002400.pdf` (object 6) has a wrong Adler-32 checksum**, and a direct /Length that overshoots `endstream` by one byte, so the searched end is used. No reader rejects the checksum: pdf.js `FlateStream` never reads it (`src/core/flate_stream.js` 6.3.289); PDFBox inflates in nowrap mode after skipping the two header bytes (`FlateFilterDecoderStream.java` lines 46-59); qpdf 12.4.2 turns "incorrect data check" into the end of the stream (`libqpdf/Pl_Flate.cc` lines 170-176: "Other PDF readers ignore this specific error"); MuPDF does the same (MuPDF 1.26.10, `source/fitz/filter-flate.c` lines 85-89, "ignoring zlib error"; behaviour only, AGPL). The header check that replaces zlib's own is pdf.js's (`flate_stream.js` lines 133-148, Apache-2.0).
  - **`pdfjs-sechandler.pdf` decrypts (qpdf, as the worker does) to a file whose object 25 is `<< /Length 0 /Filter /FlateDecode >>` with no data.** pdf.js reads it as an empty stream (`parser.js` lines 842-845, `maybeLength === 0`), and qpdf filters it to nothing (`QPDF_Stream.cc` lines 612-620).
  - **Bounds that stay, and where they come from**: the 1,024-byte window (above); the stream cap and document budget of `bounded-inflate.ts` (this project, policy 61), applied while inflating; the length object is read as at most 12 digits (`MAX_LENGTH_DIGITS`) and a length past the end of the file is not used (PDFBox `validateStreamLength`, "the end of the stream is out of range"); the later-length pass looks at each stream once, so the number of checks is bounded by the reader's object limit (500,000).
  - **Ghostscript ran a PostScript program that qpdf and Poppler read as a PDF.** A file that opens with `\n%!PS` (or `\x04%!PS`) does not match the PostScript signature at byte 0, so it passed the upload check as a `.pdf` with `%PDF-` on its second line, followed by a PostScript program that opens a string over a complete PDF. qpdf 12.4.2 and Poppler read the PDF; `compressPdf` handed the upload to Ghostscript 10.07.1, which chooses between PDF and PostScript from the first bytes, ran the program and printed its text (`-dSAFER` blocked file reads and the sandbox bounds the run). pdf.js (`checkHeader` then `moveStart`) and qpdf take the header as the start of the file and count offsets from it, so the tools that do not search for the header are handed the file from there; after that Ghostscript sees `%PDF-` at byte 0 and reads the same document. LibreOffice 26.8 imported the three variants tried (`\n%!PS`, `\x04%!PS`, `\r\n%!PS`) as the PDF, so no route is shown there; it is handed the same bytes because it also types a file by its first bytes. `-P-` is the Ghostscript default ("Makes Ghostscript not look first in the current directory for library files ... This is now the default", Ghostscript `Use` documentation) and does not affect this route; it is passed so that the profiles read as `ps2pdfwr` does (`OPTIONS="-P- -dSAFER"`).
  - **The bound on the white space a length check skips is this project's, not a reference's.** PDFBox's `validateStreamLength` skips white space and comments with `BaseParser.skipSpaces` (lines 1286-1310), which has no limit, and pdf.js (`makeStream`) and qpdf (`QPDF_objects.cc` line 1366) read the next token after the length; none of the three bounds that run. Measured before this change on the development Mac: 2,000 streams whose direct lengths end in one run of 2 MB of spaces (a 2.1 MB file) took 6.4 s to index, and the same file with lengths written after the streams took 6 ms because the reader did not check them; checking later lengths without a bound took 6.0 s. A first version of this change charged the skipped white space to the structure budget instead (128 MiB per document), which refused with a 413 a file the earlier reader read: a wrong direct `/Length 1` in front of 4 MiB of NUL bytes (white space to PDF), in each of 40 streams, ran the budget out. **The constant is 16 bytes, from the corpus**: of the 120,114 streams with a direct /Length in the 1,264 cached real-world PDFs, 120,111 have `endstream` after exactly that many bytes plus white space, and the gap is 0 bytes in 45,488 of them, 1 in 9,514 and 2 in 65,109 (a line end is one or two bytes); no stream has a larger gap, and the other 3 do not reach `endstream` at all. 16 is eight times the longest gap seen. What the bound changes against an unbounded reader: a stream whose data is followed by more than 16 bytes of white space before `endstream` (never seen in the corpus) keeps the searched end, which drops one line end and keeps the padding as data; and a comment between the data and `endstream`, which PDFBox's `skipSpaces` would skip, is not skipped (none seen).
- **Trade-offs**:
  - **An HTML page, a PostScript program behind a line break or any other text with `%PDF-` in its first kilobyte now passes the spoof check under a `.pdf` name**, as a file that starts with `%PDF-` already did. The in-process PDF reader then refuses it with a 400 when no PDF follows (tested), and a real polyglot is read as the PDF that qpdf, Poppler and pdf.js see in it. What the gate still guarantees is narrower than "no other kind reaches the PDF tools": a file that opens with a signature the registry compares with the extension is refused, and a polyglot is read as a PDF by every tool because Ghostscript and LibreOffice are handed the file from the header on, so the first bytes that made Ghostscript choose PostScript are gone. A font file (WOFF, TrueType, OpenType) passes the gate under a `.pdf` name as before; that is tracked outside this change.
  - **A truncated Flate stream is still refused.** qpdf, MuPDF, PDFBox and pdf.js keep the bytes decoded before such an end and go on. No file of the nightly run needs it (all 22 streams were whole), so the stricter answer stays until one does.
  - **Corrupt deflate data in the middle of a stream is still refused**, as qpdf (it raises "error decoding stream data" and keeps the raw bytes) and MuPDF (`fz_throw`) do; PDFBox keeps the decoded prefix and pdf.js turns it into an empty stream.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - **Searching for `%PDF-` anywhere, or line by line as PDFBox does**: the spoof check reads a bounded window and must stay a signature test, not a parser.
  - **Sniffing every file for `%PDF-` in the window** (in `sniffMimeTypeFromMagicBytes`): a text, HTML or source file that mentions `%PDF-` would then be refused under its own extension.
  - **Keeping the line end in front of `endstream` in the fallback** (pdf.js `#findStreamLength`, `parser.js` lines 654-729): the decoded bytes of every unfiltered stream found by the search would gain that line end, and those bytes include the font programs the reader hands on; resolving the length is what both pdf.js and PDFBox do first, and it leaves every other stream as it is.
  - **Inflating with `Z_SYNC_FLUSH` to keep cut-short streams**: it does not fix any of the files (their data was cut by the reader, not the producer) and would accept truncated streams without evidence that real files need it.
- **Verified by**: `tests/pdf-imperfect-input.test.ts`, red before the change (38 of 52 tests; the 14 that passed are the defences that hold before and after): the five files with bytes in front of the header (qpdf `--check` clean, Poppler page count, the spoof check, and the text of pdf to txt against `pdftotext`, word F1 at least 0.97); for each of the 22 files the decoded object against qpdf's `--filtered-stream-data`; Markdown of `govdocs1-004679.pdf` and `-001376.pdf` against `pdftotext`; `pdfjs-sechandler.pdf` decrypted, its empty stream, and pdf to rtf; synthetic files for a later /Length whose data ends in an LF or a CR right before `endstream` or in a CR before an LF line end, a later /Length that is wrong or past the end, a wrong checksum (qpdf and Poppler read it) and an empty stream; and the defences: headers at 1,019, 1,020, 1,023 and 1,025 bytes, ZIP, PE and PNG files with `%PDF-` inside and outside the window, HTML with `%PDF-` past the window and inside it, a bomb with a wrong checksum and a cut-short bomb (413 at the cap), six streams that break at their first bytes (400), and 3,000 direct or later lengths that all end in one 2 MB run of white space (read in milliseconds; before the change the direct case took 4.8 s and was not refused), 40 streams with a wrong `/Length 1` in front of 4 MiB of NUL bytes each (passes the guard in milliseconds, and the data of the streams read back whole), and the 16-byte lookahead at its edge (16 spaces and a CR LF end the data at the length; 17 spaces and a line feed fall back to the search). `tests/pdf-header-offset-tools.test.ts` covers the hand-off, red before it: a file opening with `\n%!PS` or `\x04%!PS` through `compressPdf` (the PDF's text, not the program's; before, Ghostscript failed on the program), a CR LF-prefixed file that still compresses, the helper's contract (same buffer at byte 0, a view of the same memory otherwise) and the bytes the PDF/A converter receives (a stand-in that records its input). Over all 1,264 cached real-world PDFs, the spoof verdict and the bytes the stream guard hands to pdf.js (encrypted files also after `qpdf --decrypt --static-id`) are the same before and after except for the 28 files above, and a sample of 126 conversions (21 targets, 73 files, frozen clock and UUIDs) is byte for byte the same. 57 of the 61 refused jobs now convert and pass the corpus output check; the 4 that stay are `qpdf-issue-263.pdf` (3 jobs; its header is `%PDFn` and qpdf cannot find its trailer) and `pdfjs-sechandler.pdf` to pdf, which now stops at OCR because the page's only text is a form field appearance. The parity families `document`, `compression` and `pdf-ops` (`--parity --quality-only --quick` on the development Mac, before and after) evaluate 130 rows with none failing, and no row moves beyond the one-byte run-to-run noise of `pdf-ops/watermark.pdf->pdf/bytes` and `compress.pdf->pdf/bytes`.
- **Revisit when**: a real file has a Flate stream that is cut short or breaks in the middle and the readers recover text from it (then keep the decoded prefix, as qpdf and MuPDF do with `Z_SYNC_FLUSH`, with the same caps and a warning); a file has its /Length object inside an object stream (the later-length pass runs before object streams are unpacked); or a file opens with its header past the first 1,024 bytes and a reader opens it.

## 74. PostScript files the real-world run refuses: bitmap-font pages for DXF, and two files Ghostscript stops on

- **Status**: Implemented (this change). One message changes in `isBitmapGlyph` and `renderNodeAtDepth` (`svg-geometry.ts`), and `bench/realworld/known-refusals.json` explains 14 files. No conversion that worked stops working and none that was refused starts. The DXF part is superseded by [77](#77-bitmap-font-text-in-dxf-the-characters-as-text-in-a-stock-font-from-the-pdf-text-layer): the DXF writer now writes bitmap-font characters as TEXT, three of the twelve files convert and the other nine are listed for other content; EMF and WMF still refuse the glyph as below.
- **Decision**:
  - **A DXF (and an EMF or WMF) is still not written from a page that draws a character of a bitmap font.** Poppler draws such a page, a PostScript file set in dvips Type 3 fonts of 1-bit pictures, with cairo's SVG surface, which writes each character as `<rect mask="url(#mask-N)"/>` whose mask holds the glyph picture. The geometry reader now recognises that shape (a `<rect>` whose mask holds only `<image>` elements or `<use>` of them) and refuses it as `SVG bitmap glyph (a character of a bitmap font, drawn as a picture) is not supported`, where it said `SVG mask "url(#mask-0)" is not supported`. Every other mask, a mask on a path or a group, a mask that draws a shape, an empty, missing or self-referring mask, keeps the plain mask refusal.
  - **Two files stay refused in every target, and the nightly gate lists them as explained**: `govdocs1-002500.ps` (Ghostscript `invalidaccess` in `put`) and `govdocs1-006458.ps` (`limitcheck` in `setcolorspace`). Safe mode is not loosened and no argument is added; the interpreter runs as before (`-dSAFER`).
  - **Twelve bitmap-font files are listed as explained refusals for DXF**, with the number of Type 3 fonts on page one and what else the pages hold.
- **Why**:
  - **The nightly run of `58369e26` refused 18 PostScript jobs.** 12 were `ps` to `dxf` with the mask message (12 files), and 6 were the interpreter's rejection (2 files, each refused for `pdf`, `dxf` and `avif`).
  - **The mask is a bitmap glyph, not a soft mask.** All 1,081 masked elements on the first pages of the 12 files are `<rect mask>` elements, with the picture as `<image id="source-N">` in `<defs>`. cairo 1.18.0 `src/cairo-svg-surface.c` writes exactly that for a bitmap font in `_cairo_svg_document_emit_bitmap_glyph_data` (lines 1349 to 1470; the mask at line 1424, the rectangle at lines 1447 to 1453). `pdffonts` lists Type 3 fonts on page one of all 12 files (2 to 15 each), and `pdftotext` reads 20 to 4,202 characters from each page measured that holds nothing but those glyphs (17 pages of 5 files).
  - **The premise that dropping the masks makes the files convert is false.** Before settling, a prototype dropped the bitmap glyphs and the pictures, ignored the pen properties the DXF writer never writes (dash arrays, strokes under a skew) and allowed pages without geometry, and every page of only 2 of the 12 files could be written. Pages kept, of pages in the file (the rest are pages of nothing but bitmap glyphs, pages with a clip path, or pages over the 5 MiB SVG limit)

    | File | Pages kept | File | Pages kept |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `-000097` | 0 of 2 | `-004087` | 5 of 5 |
    | `-000730` | 1 of 2 | `-005434` | 10 of 22 |
    | `-000773` | 23 of 24 | `-006007` | 0 of 4 |
    | `-001048` | 12 of 15 | `-006137` | 25 of 66 |
    | `-002433` | 4 of 5 | `-006955` | 6 of 8 |
    | `-002956` | 33 of 33 | `-003229` | 10 of 20 |

    The pages that remain are written with their rules and without their text, and a page of bitmap glyphs is written as an empty drawing. A DXF that looks like the page and lacks its words is a worse answer than a refusal that says why.
  - **What the reference writers do with a bitmap.** Inkscape `share/extensions/dxf_outlines.py` (GPL, behaviour only) draws only `PathElement`, `Rectangle`, `Line`, `Circle` and `Ellipse` (`process_shape`, line 265 to 267) and never reads a mask, a clip or an image, so the text of a bitmap page is simply absent. pstoedit 4.02 (GPL, behaviour only) declares its three DXF drivers `noimage` and without clipping (`src/drvdxf.cpp` lines 1711 to 1757; `drvbase.h` lines 596 to 598 print "show_image called, although backend does not support images"), and replaces a bitmap font with a stock font written as TEXT (`doc/pstoedit.tex` lines 328 to 334, and the `-nfr` option in `doc/pstoedit.htm`). The installed LibreOffice has no DXF export (`soffice --convert-to dxf` answers "no export filter"). No reference keeps the pixels; one drops them without a word and one writes the characters again as text, which this converter cannot yet do for a bitmap font.
  - **`govdocs1-002500.ps` (WordPerfect 6.1) stops at the font for page 10 of 27.** The font programs fill the Encoding array of a Type 3 font with integers (`0 1 255{Encoding exch 0 put}for`, where `Widths` was meant) and then add glyphs after `definefont` with `Encoding 1 /1 put`. The same bytes with that one line filling `Widths` convert to 27 pages, so the line is the cause (measured). Ghostscript 10.07.1 `psi/zbfont.c` lines 782 to 842 rebuild an Encoding that holds integers (Distiller's behaviour, bug 692681, as the source comment says) with `ialloc_ref_array(&penc, r_type_attrs(pencoding) & a_readonly, ...)` at line 817, and `a_readonly` is `a_read + a_execute` (`psi/iref.h` lines 351 to 355), so the copy has no write bit when the original had one and the next `put` is `invalidaccess` (read from the source; the line at 792 in 10.02.1 and 10.04.0, and 908 on the development branch, are the same). The error is the same with `-dNOSAFER`.
  - **`govdocs1-006458.ps` (QuickDraw GX driver) stops at the first colour space of page 1.** `MakeCalibratedRGBSpace` declares `/RangeABC [0 65535 0 65535 0 65535]` and the same for `/RangeLMN`. Ghostscript accepts a CIE parameter of at most 10000 (`psi/zcolor.c` line 64 `MAX_CIE_RANGE`, checked in `get_cie_param_array`, lines 2305 to 2323, which returns `limitcheck`; the same constant in 10.02.1, 10.04.0 and the development branch). With the two ranges set to 9999 the file converts to its 24 pages (measured). The error is the same with `-dNOSAFER`.
  - **Both interpreter files are refused whole.** The interpreter stops with a non-zero exit and writes the pages before the fault (9 of 27 for `-002500`), which is not a conversion of the file, so the refusal is right and no partial PDF is returned.
- **Trade-offs**:
  - **The 12 DXF jobs stay refused.** The text of a bitmap-font document is the document, and the only way to keep it is to write it again (TEXT with a placement rule, or traced outlines), which the references do differently and which has no source here that this change could follow. Three files are mostly outline fonts (`-000773`, `-002956`, `-004087`); their few bitmap glyphs and two pictures are content too, and DXF stays a format that does not drop pictures without a word (policy 42).
  - **The same files convert to every other target** (PDF, PNG, SVG, EPS, PostScript, the raster formats), where the glyphs are kept.
  - **The two Ghostscript files are refused until the worker image carries a Ghostscript that accepts them.** The converter does not rewrite the program, which would be this project's own parser of PostScript.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - **Dropping bitmap glyphs and pictures as Inkscape's writer does**: the prototype above shows what is left, rules without words.
  - **Writing the words as TEXT from the PDF text layer, as pstoedit does with a stock font**: Poppler reads the text of all 12 files, but the size, baseline and width of each word would be this project's own rules, and the characters of an outline-font word beside a bitmap word would have to be told apart. A backlog item, with the reference written first.
  - **Tracing the glyph pictures into outlines (potrace, GPL, behaviour only)**: tens of thousands of vertices per page for text that was one font, against the 500,000-vertex document budget of the geometry reader.
  - **`-dNOSAFER` or a patched copy of the program**: safe mode is not involved in either fault (the error is the same with it), and a patched copy is a PostScript rewriter.
  - **A refusal text per page or per font**: the message says what the content is and the known-refusal rows carry the file facts.
- **Verified by**: `tests/svg-bitmap-glyph.test.ts`, red before the change (5 of 11 tests; the 6 that passed are the plain mask refusals that stay): the DXF, EMF and geometry-reader refusals of a page built like Poppler's; every other mask kept as the mask refusal; hostile pages (a mask that refers to itself, 30,000 copies of a glyph refused at the first, a mask of 30,000 pictures read once, a long run of pictures ended by a shape) refused inside 2 seconds. `tests/postscript-real-file-refusals.test.ts`, red before the change (26 of 42): each of the 12 files refused for DXF with the bitmap glyph named, converted to PDF with a Type 3 font and more than 100 characters of text read by `pdffonts` and `pdftotext`; the two Ghostscript files refused for PDF and DXF with the interpreter's own error, and converted to 27 and 24 pages once the one line or the two ranges are changed; every file matched by its row of `known-refusals.json`.
- **Revisit when**: the converter writes bitmap-font text as DXF TEXT or as outlines from a reference that fixes size, baseline and width; DXF is allowed to leave pictures out (then also the pen properties it ignores); or the worker image carries a Ghostscript that reads either file (then remove its row).

## 75. Page images: Poppler's rasteriser and page counter start from the launcher helper

- **Status**: Implemented (issue #735, epic #759), decided 2026-10-23 (QA request: bring `vector/shapes.ai->png/throughput`, 0.894 [0.781, 0.923] over 6 pairs in the nightly of 2026-10-22, to the reference). It applies entry 31 to the programs entry 34 left to a direct start.
- **Decision**:
  - **`pdftoppm` (PNG pages through PPM, JPEG, TIFF and the PNG fallback for a page above the raw-size limit) and `pdfinfo` (the page count) start through `launchSandboxedBinary`**, the launcher helper of entry 31, instead of from the worker process. The helper is used when the fork it saves costs more than it adds (`launchPays`: the process holds more than 112 MiB), and a direct start is the fallback, as for `qpdf`, `gs` and `rsvg-convert`.
  - **Nothing else changes.** The command lines, the user and network namespace of each program, the file-size limit of the raw page, the time limit, the typed errors (password, damaged document, page above the limit), the batches, the early page count, the PPM encoder and the output bytes are those of entry 34 and entry 67. `pdftoppm` writes files and prints nothing, so the launcher is told to expect no output (`expectedOutputBytes` 0); `pdfinfo` prints a few hundred bytes (4 KiB).
- **Why**:
  - **Where the time of one conversion goes** (`shapes.ai`, 2,500 bytes, 400 x 300 points at 72 dpi; 4-core arm64 VM, Ubuntu 24.04, Poppler 24.02, Node 24.22; medians of 60 to 80 alternating runs):

    | Stage | Time |
    |---|---|
    | Reference command `pdftoppm -png -singlefile -scale-to-x 400` | 15.3 to 16.3 ms |
    | Of it, the program drawing a PPM page (what ours runs) | 5.2 ms (7.15 ms in `spawnSync`) |
    | Of it, libpng at `Z_BEST_COMPRESSION` | about 9 ms |
    | Ours, before: whole call | 8.8 to 9.2 ms |
    | Before, the spawn calls on the worker's thread (`pdftoppm` 0.8, `pdfinfo` 0.6) | 1.4 ms |
    | Before, `pdftoppm` behind `prlimit`, spawn to exit | 4.5 ms |
    | Before, `pdfinfo`, beside it | 2.9 ms, off the critical path |
    | Before, after the exit: read the page, PNG encode (1.5 ms), write, remove | 1.95 ms |
    | Ours, after (any resident size) | 8.4 to 8.6 ms |

    Ours already runs at 1.6 to 1.7 times the reference on the VM, where the whole reference is 15 to 16 ms. The row falls under 1.0 only on the nightly runner, where one night in four measured 0.894 and the three before it 0.98, 1.19 and 1.21 (each night's own noise: the lowest night ran the reference at 49 ms against 24 to 33 ms on the others). The part of ours that grows with the machine is what the reference does not pay: two program starts from a large process.
  - **The start of a program from a large process.** Node starts a program with `fork()` (entry 58: neither `vfork` nor `posix_spawn` on Linux). Measured on the VM with a heap of small objects (4 KiB pages, as a real worker has): an asynchronous start of `/bin/true` takes 0.40, 0.56, 0.85, 1.50 and 2.06 ms at 66, 209, 422, 809 and 1,203 MB resident. Ours starts two programs for every call (the early `pdftoppm` and `pdfinfo`) on the one thread that also decodes the page. The benchmark process holds 317 MB during the vector family on the VM, and the nightly run holds every family's earlier work in one process. A job in a worker holds a few hundred megabytes.
  - **Effect.** Whole call of ours at 60, 300 and 1,000 MB resident, before and after, two alternating pairs each: 8.9 and 8.7 to 8.5 and 8.4 ms at 60 MB (the helper is not used below 112 MiB, so this is noise), 9.0 and 9.2 to 8.5 and 8.8 at 300 MB, 10.0 and 10.2 to 8.4 and 8.4 at 1,000 MB. After the change the time does not depend on the size of the process.
  - **Parity bench, `--parity --speed-only --family vector`, head against base, interleaved in two processes** (6 runs of 18 pairs each, medians; the testbed is shared with other work, so the ranges are wide): `shapes.ai->png` 1.105 (runs from 1.090 to 1.122, upper bounds 1.17 to 1.29), against 1.70 ours / reference. The other six vector rows did not move (0.996 to 1.009). Family `document`: `pptx->png` 1.009 and `xlsx->png` 1.001 head against base (18 pairs; the office suite's 300 ms is most of those rows, and a document of 7 pages or fewer is one batch).
  - **Same pictures.** `--parity --quality-only --family vector` with `ORACLE_STRICT_MODE=1`: 264 rows at or above the reference, 0 failing, 0 moved against the baseline; `shapes.ai->png` SSIM 0.9987 and PSNR 47.482 dB, both equal to the reference's. Family `document` (without epubcheck, which the VM lacks): 268 rows evaluated, 0 failing, the same counts as the base; `pptx->png` word recall 0.82979 and `xlsx->png` 0.97041, both equal to the reference. Base and head write the same bytes for `shapes.ai`, a 19-page document as a PNG ZIP and as a JPEG ZIP, a page selection (3 to 5), the first page only, and four text PDFs (9 outputs, and the same 4 refusals of a page the document lacks; `sha256` of every entry), and the PNG equals `pdftoppm -png` pixel for pixel (`tests/render-launcher.test.ts`).
  - **What the reference does differently** (Poppler 24.02, read for this change): `utils/pdftoppm.cc` draws every page on one thread (`savePageSlice`, lines 283 to 339), writes the page with `SplashBitmap::writeImgFile` and `goo/PNGWriter.cc` line 123 sets `Z_BEST_COMPRESSION`, which is the 9 ms of its 15; `lastPage` is clamped to the document's page count at lines 516 to 518; with `-scale-to-x` the resolution is `72 * x / page width` (lines 652 to 667). Ours draws the same Splash page at the same resolution and compresses it at level 5 with adaptive row filters (entry 34); nothing about the resolution, anti-aliasing or colour changes.
- **Trade-offs**:
  - **The helper's own cost**: about 0.1 to 0.25 ms of wake-ups per program (entry 31), one helper process of about 50 MB per worker (already there when SVG, PostScript or `qpdf` jobs ran), and a direct start for the first requests after the worker starts, until the helper has said it is ready.
  - **The x64 runner is not reproduced here.** The VM refuses unprivileged user namespaces (an AppArmor setting that the runner switches off), so the `unshare -r -n` in front of each program, 0.27 to 0.46 ms in entry 58, is not in these profiles. The change removes two forks per call whatever the namespaces cost; the nightly row after the merge is the check.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - **Reading the page count from the first render and skipping `pdfinfo`** (the batch of pages 1 to 8 comes back with fewer pages than asked, so the document is that short): the count is exact, but `pdfinfo` runs beside `pdftoppm` on another core, so on this VM it is not on the critical path and nothing could be measured; and without an early `pdfinfo` a document of more pages loses the other batches for the length of the first (the 85-slide deck of entry 67 would draw its first 7 pages alone), unless a guess about the size of the file decides.
  - **`pdftoppm` writing the PPM to standard output, without the `prlimit` wrapper**: 5.15 against 5.36 ms for the program (file with `prlimit`), 4 percent, and the batch of up to eight pages would be held twice in memory against the limit of one page.
  - **A PNG encoder without adaptive row filters, or a lower level**: 0.63 ms against 1.54 ms for the benchmark page, and the file is larger on photographs (entry 34, QA decides the trade). Threads of the image library: 1.43 ms on one thread and 1.47 ms on four.
  - **A runner mode that makes the namespaces, applies the limits and executes the program** (entry 60, "revisit when"): it would remove the fork, the `unshare` and the `prlimit` starts together, about 1 ms of the 8.4 on the VM, at the price of extending a 900-line native program with fuzzing and a security review; not done for a row that is already at 1.7 on the VM.
- **Verified by**: `tests/render-launcher.test.ts` (PDF to PNG: the helper starts `pdftoppm` and `pdfinfo` once each, red before the change with 0 programs started, and the pixels equal `pdftoppm -png`; a document Poppler cannot open is still the page counter's typed error), `tests/render-limits-stubbed-sandbox.test.ts` (the raw-size fallback in both ways, with the launcher stubbed, and the launcher told to expect no output), `tests/poppler-ppm-png.test.ts` (pages, batches, processes at once, the errors) and the `vector` and `document` parity rows above.
- **Revisit when**: the nightly `vector/shapes.ai->png/throughput` still reads under 1.0 after the merge (then profile on x64 with user namespaces enabled), Node starts programs with `vfork` or `posix_spawn` on Linux, or the native runner takes other programs.

## 76. WAVE sources for audio conversions: the demuxer cuts the file into about 32 packets

- **Status**: Implemented (issue #642, epic #759), decided 2026-10-23 (QA request: bring `audio/pteraxys-part1.flac->flac/throughput`, 0.967 [0.961, 0.976] over 50 pairs in the nightly of 2026-10-22, to the reference).
- **Decision**:
  - **A conversion from a WAVE file that its header describes to an audio target that only changes container and codec passes `-max_size` to ffmpeg's WAV demuxer**: the file is cut into packets of a thirty-second part of its samples, never below the demuxer's 4096 bytes and never above 128 KiB (`src/lib/conversions/media-wav-input.ts`, `addWavPacketSize`). The option is declared from 1024 to 1 << 22 bytes and the demuxer rounds the packet down to whole sample frames (`libavformat/wavdec.c` line 76 and lines 775 to 782 of n6.1.1).
  - **The default packets stay for** a trimmed file, a request that sets a sample rate, a channel count, a volume or a loudness target (any `-ar`, `-ac`, `-filter:a` or `-af`), a 16-bit target of a deeper source (dither), libopus from a rate it does not code (`-ar 48000`), a header that does not describe the samples, an input that is not a WAVE file, and a build that is not FFmpeg 4.4 to 8.0 (a development build, 8.1 and 9.x included). The version comes from the `-version` run that `chooseResampler` already makes once per binary (`probeFfmpegBuild`).
  - **Nothing else changes.** The encoder, its settings, the metadata copied from the file, the sandbox, the time limit and the typed errors are those of entries 3, 54 and 66; the encoders write the same bytes.
- **Why**:
  - **Where the time goes** (`pteraxys-part1.flac` cut to 12 s, 24-bit stereo, a 3,175,376-byte WAVE; 4-core arm64 VM, Ubuntu 24.04, FFmpeg 6.1.1, Node 24.22; medians of 40 runs, ours and the reference command alternating in one process, load average 0.7 to 0.8 before each run):

    | Stage | Ours, before | Ours, after |
    |---|---|---|
    | Header read, temporary directory, input written to it (0.75 ms of the 1.0) | 1.0 ms | 1.0 ms |
    | `spawn` call (fork of a 200 MB process and exec) | 1.1 ms | 1.1 ms |
    | ffmpeg from exec to exit | 60.7 ms | 39.1 ms |
    | Output renamed, read (0.49 ms), temporary files removed (0.6 ms) | 0.85 ms | 0.85 ms |
    | Whole call | 63.7 ms | 42.1 ms |
    | The reference command (`spawnSync`, input read in place), same process | 62.1 ms | 61.9 ms |

    The row sat at 0.967 because ours wrote and read back 5.9 MB the reference never touched (about 2 ms of file work around the same ffmpeg run), and on the runner it also pays the `unshare -r -n` that the VM refuses (entry 58, 0.27 to 0.46 ms). The runner's own numbers in the nightly of 10-22: 26.98 MB/s against 27.74 MB/s, 117.7 ms against 114.5 ms.
  - **The limiter is not the copies. It is how ffmpeg 6.1 feeds the encoder.** `ffmpeg -version` alone takes 22 ms (214 shared libraries, 388,854 relative relocations); the rest of the 62 ms is a pipeline of four threads (demuxer, decoder, filter graph, encoder) that exchange one packet at a time through queues. The WAV demuxer's packet is 4096 bytes, so 12 s are 776 packets, and `/usr/bin/time` counts 2,440 voluntary context switches for the run, 229 with 99,225-byte packets. The same encode with `-filter_threads 1` takes 8 ms longer, which is the share of the pipeline that overlaps. FFmpeg 7.0 made the demuxer's default 100 ms of audio for this reason (commit 44b2769619, "demux bigger packets, which is more efficient", and `ff_pcm_default_packet_size` in `libavformat/pcm.c` lines 29 to 54 of n7.1); this entry applies the same remedy to the builds before it, with a larger packet where the file is long enough.
  - **Why 32 packets and 128 KiB** (12 s, ffmpeg wall time, medians of 15 runs, packet bytes 4096, 8192, 16384, 32768, 65536, 131072): 24-bit stereo to FLAC 63.5, 53.3, 47.2, 44.5, 43.1, 42.4 ms; 16-bit stereo (2.1 MB) 54.1, 46.6, 42.6, 41.0, 40.8, 41.3 ms, and 52.9 ms with one packet of 1 MiB, which leaves two packets and no overlap; 5.1 at 48 kHz 118.0, 94.6, 83.4, 77.6, 75.3, 73.5 ms; 24-bit stereo at 96 kHz 107.9, 85.3, 74.0, 69.3, 66.5, 64.6 ms. Mono at 8 kHz (192 KB) is 26 to 27 ms at every size. Past 128 KiB nothing falls, and a file of two or three packets loses the overlap, so a packet is never more than a thirty-second part of the file.
  - **Result** (`--parity --speed-only --family audio` with the base beside the head, 24 pairs a row, the VM shared with one other benchmark): `pteraxys-part1.flac->flac` 1.429 [1.401, 1.449] of the reference against 0.968 for the base, head against base 1.468; the other FLAC rows 1.027 to 1.672 head against base (`edge-surround-5.1.wav` 1.672, `sqam-65` 1.344, `sqam-49` 1.326, `sqam-27` 1.294, `music.wav` 1.116, `speech.wav` 1.027); AAC rows 1.010 to 1.116 (`edge-surround-5.1.wav` 1.116, `sqam-49` 1.099, `pteraxys` 1.088, `sqam-65` 1.063, `sqam-27` 1.060, `music.wav` 1.029, `speech.wav` 1.010). The Opus rows did not move (0.991 to 1.012): they resample, and keep the default packets.
  - **Same files.** The five WAVE samples (24-bit stereo, 16-bit stereo, 8 kHz mono, 5.1, 96 kHz 24-bit) converted to FLAC by base and head are byte for byte equal; `flac -t` accepts them, the MD5 in each file equals the MD5 of the decoded samples and of the source samples, and they are 77 bytes larger than the reference command's file, which is the tags of the source that the product copies and the reference's `-map_metadata -1` drops. Through the product, 5 sources, 8 targets (FLAC, WAV, MP3, AAC, M4A, Opus, Ogg, AIFF) and 6 option sets (none, a rate, mono, a volume, a loudness target, an end time) give 235 conversions (five are refused: Opus has no 22.05 kHz). With the new packets and with the old ones every one decodes to the same samples, 180 are equal byte for byte, and the 55 that are not are Ogg and Opus files, whose stream serial is random (two runs of one command differ as well). 32 of the 235 use the option. The same matrix with the option on every conversion, before the rule left out the ones with a filter, rate or channel change, had 32 conversions that decoded to other samples: the dither of a 16-bit reduction, soxr resampling, `volume` on 16-bit samples and `loudnorm` carry state from one chunk to the next, so they are equally correct and not equal.
  - **What the reference does differently**: nothing in the encoder. `ffmpeg -i in.wav -c:a flac -compression_level 5` runs the same `libavcodec/flacenc.c` (level 5 is block time 105 ms, prediction order 8, partition order 8: lines 356, 368 and 385; no frame or slice threading: capabilities at lines 1756 to 1758) and the same four-thread pipeline over 4096-byte packets. It reads the input in place, writes the output where the caller asked, and states `-map_metadata -1`. A cut of the input at `-to` falls on a packet boundary, the side effect that commit 44b2769619 names for `-c:a copy -t`, which is why a trimmed file keeps the default packets.
- **Trade-offs**:
  - **FFmpeg 8.1 and 9.x keep the default packets.** From the development branch after 8.0 the command line tool gives an encoder without a frame size the length of the first frame (`fftools/ffmpeg_enc.c`, `enc_ctx->frame_size = frame->nb_samples`), which the FLAC encoder takes as its block size (`libavcodec/flacenc.c` lines 411 to 421): FFmpeg 9.0.2 writes FLAC blocks of 4096 samples with its default packets, 16537 with packets of 66150 bytes and 32768 with 131072 bytes (148,790, 142,986 and 140,993 bytes for a 6 s sine), and 6.1.1 writes 4608. So the option changes the file there, and the range is the releases where it does not. The Mac development machine (9.0.2) therefore times the old packets.
  - **A conversion that resamples, mixes, filters or trims is not faster** (Opus from 44.1 kHz, a sample-rate request, a volume, a loudness target, a start or end time). The decoded samples would be correct either way; they would not be the same samples, and the entry does not trade that.
  - **A little more memory in ffmpeg**: the largest resident size of the 12 s FLAC run is 54.1 MB against 53.5 MB.
- **Alternatives rejected** (each measured on the VM, ours/reference of this row or the time it saves; two runs of one setting differ by 0.3 to 0.5 ms):
  - **Starting ffmpeg through the launcher helper of entry 31**: paired difference of the whole call between -0.56 and +0.45 ms in the quiet runs of 90 to 120 pairs at 200 to 730 MB resident (the fork of a 200 MB process is 0.58 ms and the helper's round trip costs most of it), so noise; on this row the program starts once and no probe starts beside it (`ffmpeg -version` and `-encoders` run once per binary).
  - **The sandbox's temporary files**: the input through the child's standard input (0.0 to 0.7 ms, the write moves into the start-up of ffmpeg but the child is slower for it), the files on tmpfs (write 0.38 against 0.28 ms, no change in the whole), closing the last handle of the large files on a pool thread (-0.7 ms in the first quiet run, -0.4 and -0.1 in the two later ones).
  - **Environment of the child**: `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` (the nightly job sets it, the sanitized environment of the child drops it), `MALLOC_TOP_PAD_`, `glibc.malloc.hugetlb=1`, `LANG` and `LC_ALL`: 62.4 to 63.0 ms against 62.6, nothing.
  - **A smaller command line**: `-hide_banner -nostdin -v error`, no `-map`, no `-sn -dn`: no difference.
  - **The in-repo FLAC encoder (entry 54, "two routes for one target")**: 77 ms for the 12 s file on one thread against 62 for ffmpeg; 2,678,626 bytes against 2,688,713.
  - **The reference `flac` 1.4.3 program** (`flac -5`, the oracle of the FLAC tests): 18.6 ms for this file and 2,688,007 bytes, against 40.3 ms after this entry. It is not an engine of the product (`flac` is not in the worker image), would write other bytes and no copied tags, and is a second route for one target, so the change is QA's call; libFLAC 1.5.0 can also encode on several threads (`stream_encoder.c` lines 414, 1134 to 1136, 2151 to 2165).
  - **A spare ffmpeg started ahead of the request** (the 22 ms start-up is the shared floor): one spare per command line, so only the default FLAC conversion, and one process more per worker; not built.
  - **Packets larger than 128 KiB, or one packet per file**: no gain past 128 KiB, and a file of two packets runs as long as one of 776.
- **Verified by**: `tests/media-wav-packet-size.test.ts` (the packet size for the sizes of the table, the release table, the arguments of FLAC, MP3, AAC, M4A and Ogg, each case that keeps the default packets, the loudness passes, and the FLAC, AAC, MP3 and Vorbis files of the reference encoder equal with and without the option), `tests/media-audio-encode-overhead.test.ts` (the minimal probe is unchanged for short files), and the `audio` throughput rows above.
- **Revisit when**: FFmpeg's FLAC block size stops following the first frame (then 8.1 and later join the range), the Opus rows can be shown to decode to the same samples from larger packets, the worker image ships libFLAC's program, or the nightly `audio/pteraxys-part1.flac->flac/throughput` falls under 1.0 again.

## 77. Bitmap-font text in DXF: the characters as TEXT in a stock font, from the PDF text layer

- **Status**: Implemented (issue #783, PostScript row), decided 2026-10-23 (QA request in issue #783: write bitmap-font characters as DXF TEXT in a substitute font, as the reference converter does by default). Supersedes the DXF part of entry 74.
- **Decision**:
  - **A page that draws characters of a bitmap font is written as DXF, with those characters as TEXT.** Poppler draws such a character as a picture (entry 74). The DXF writer no longer refuses it: it sets the picture aside with the glyph origin on the baseline and the extent of the picture, and writes the characters that the PDF's text layer gives for those glyphs, read by the in-process PDF reader of entries 49 and 70 (one run of shown text per item). Everything else on the page is written as before, and outline-font text stays the geometry it was.
  - **One TEXT per run of the text layer.** A glyph belongs to the run on its baseline whose advance holds the glyph origin, to within 0.01 pt. A run that holds glyphs is written once: style STANDARD (font `txt`, the default style of the format), left end on the baseline, fitted to the run's advance width (group 72 = 5, Fit), at the cap height of its font. A run that holds no glyph picture is not written.
  - **The height is the cap height, measured on the pictures**: the median top above the baseline of the pictures of the font's capital letters on the page, read where a run has one glyph per character. A font with no capital letter on the page takes the top of the tallest glyph of each run (the full picture for a run drawn wholly below the baseline).
  - **Only horizontal text.** A run of bitmap-font text that is angled or vertical refuses the page.
  - **A glyph without text is written as `#` over its picture**: a glyph that no run holds, a glyph that only a run of white space holds, and a glyph the reader finds no Unicode value for inside a run (the reader keeps it in place as `#` for this writer, where entry 49 leaves it out of text outputs). When such glyphs outnumber the glyphs that have text, the page is refused, with the rule the text reader applies to a page whose glyphs mostly have no Unicode value (entry 49).
  - **TEXT strings.** Printable ASCII is written as it is; `%` as `%%%` and `^` as `^ `, so that no reader takes them for a special code; a backslash and every other character of the Basic Multilingual Plane as `\U+XXXX`; a control character and a character beyond the plane as `#`. A string longer than 255 characters refuses the page.
  - **Bounds.** The glyphs of a page are bounded by the geometry reader's 100,000 rendered elements (a glyph is drawn as a `<use>`, its group and its rectangle, so at most 33,333 a page), and the TEXT entities by the glyphs. Whether a rectangle is a glyph is decided once per element, so a glyph used 30,000 times whose mask holds 30,000 pictures reads the mask once. A glyph finds its run by binary search over the runs of its baseline, so 200,000 runs on one baseline cost no pairwise comparison. The text layer is read once per document and only when a page draws a glyph picture, for the rendered pages, under the reader's limits (500,000 items and 16,777,216 characters a document, 60 s).
  - **EMF and WMF, and an SVG converted to DXF directly, still refuse a bitmap glyph**: they have no text layer to take the characters from.
- **Why**:
  - **The reference converter writes these characters as text** (pstoedit 4.02, GPL, read for behaviour only). Its prologue shows text as text for Type 1, 2 and 42 fonts and for a Type 3 font with a `/BitMaps` entry, which dvips writes (`src/pstoedit.pro` line 2583), and replaces a non-standard font with its default font, Courier (line 163), at the size that makes the fragment as wide as in the original font (lines 2307 to 2311; `doc/pstoedit.tex` lines 294 to 299). A character it cannot map becomes `#` (same lines). Its DXF driver writes each fragment as TEXT with the start point, the font size as height, the string and the angle, and no style (`src/drvdxf.cpp` lines 970 to 1000; "we need a STYLE table for this", line 995). The other reference writer drops the characters (entry 74).
  - **Fit keeps the reference's width rule without knowing the reader's font.** The reference sizes Courier so that each fragment keeps its width; DXF's Fit alignment makes the reader stretch the text between the two points at the given height (ezdxf, MIT: `entities/text.py` `set_placement`, lines 225 to 270; the drawing add-on computes the stretch in `addons/drawing/text.py`, lines 194 to 205). A reader draws `txt` with whatever font it maps it to, so a width given as a factor would be right for one font only. Per run, because the runs are the fragments the PDF shows (a word, or the part of one between two kerns); per line, the words would drift inside the line in the reader's font; per glyph, a word would be split into letters.
  - **The height of a DXF TEXT is the cap height.** ezdxf takes `dxf.height` as the cap height when it draws (`addons/drawing/text.py` `_get_cap_height`, lines 142 to 144) and measures a font's cap height as the top of `A` (`fonts/ttfonts.py`, lines 97 to 110). The reference writes the em size there, which draws the text taller than the original by the ratio of em to cap height. The Type 3 fonts carry no metrics to read the cap height from: Ghostscript writes `/FontBBox [0 0 1 -1]` and `/FontMatrix [1 0 0 -1 0 0]` for them, and each glyph is a picture in pixels (measured in the PDF of `govdocs1-000097.ps`). The median of the capital letters rather than `A` alone: subsets often lack `A` (5 of the 14 fonts on the two pages of that file have no capital letter at all), and `A` overshoots the flat capitals (8.42 pt against 8.16 pt for H, E, I, T and the others on its page 1).
  - **The PDF reader's runs rather than Poppler's word boxes.** The reader places a glyph where Poppler's SVG places it (on the twelve files a glyph origin and the start of its run differ by at most 0.0012 pt, on `002956`, against the 0.01 pt allowed), its runs carry the font for the cap height, and it is already in the process with its limits. `pdftotext -bbox` gives words without a font, in boxes built from a default ascent and descent for a font without metrics (the boxes of these files put the baseline 0.95 / 1.3 of the way down, measured on page 1 of `000097`).
  - **`#`, not U+FFFD.** Entry 7 writes U+FFFD where the target's fonts can draw it; the fonts of a CAD reader (`txt` and the shape fonts) have no glyph for it, and `#` is the reference's mark. A text output leaves such a glyph out (entry 49) because its text stands alone; in a drawing the picture's place would be blank.
  - **The twelve files of entry 74** (macOS, Poppler 26.09, Ghostscript 10.07.1; the evaluation renders each DXF page with ezdxf 1.4.5's drawing add-on and each PostScript page with Ghostscript at 100 dpi and compares the ink box of every Poppler word in the two renders; word recall is the share of Poppler's words, on pages set only in Type 3 fonts, that the TEXT on their baseline holds, a glyph Poppler gives no Unicode value counting as `#`; agreement is the share of TEXT strings Poppler reads at their place):

    | File | Pages | TEXT (of them `#`) | ezdxf audit errors | Word recall | Agreement | Word ink-box IoU, mean / median / share at least 0.5 |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | `govdocs1-000097.ps` | 2 | 1,045 (52) | 0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 0.90 to 0.91 / 0.95 / 0.98 to 1.0 |
    | `govdocs1-006007.ps` | 4 | 874 (50) | 0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 0.82 to 0.86 / 0.85 to 0.88 / 0.99 to 1.0 |
    | `govdocs1-000773.ps` | 24 | 87 (0) | 0 | 1.0 (page 24, the one set only in Type 3 fonts) | 1.0 | 0.83 / 0.86 / 1.0 (page 24) |

    The other nine stay refused for what else their pages draw, and `known-refusals.json` says which pages: dashed lines (`002433`, `002956`, `003229`, `005434`, `006955`, and pages of `001048`, `004087`, `006137`), strokes under a non-uniform scale (`000730`, `001048`, `006137`, and pages of `002956`, `006955`), clips that are not the page (13 pages of `006137`), pictures (`004087`, entry 42), SVG pages over the 5 MiB the geometry reader reads (2 pages of `003229`). On every page that the writer reads, their bitmap-font text is placed, but for page 28 of `002956`, whose 13 glyph pictures the text layer reads as spaces.
  - **Cost.** The text layer of a document is read once, in 25 to 101 ms for these files (median of 5, the first run of a thread about 0.8 s more, macOS host).
- **Trade-offs**:
  - **The characters are in a stock font**: Greek, mathematical symbols and accents lose their shape, and a ligature or symbol whose glyph has no Unicode value is `#` (on `000097`, 52 of the 1,045 TEXT strings are a lone `#` and some words hold one, as `fi`, `fl` and `ffi` of the TeX fonts and the itemize bullets have none: "#tted" for "fitted"). The PDF's text layer decides the characters: a glyph that it reads as a space inside a run stays a space (a `c` on page 1 of `006007`, which Poppler reads as a space too).
  - **Rotated bitmap-font text refuses the page.** None of the twelve files has any.
  - **A PDF opened with a password**: the text layer reader is not given the password, so a page that draws bitmap-font characters is refused as encrypted where Poppler drew it.
  - **A page with a text layer that has no text for most of its glyph pictures stays refused**, as before.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - **TEXT left-aligned at the em size, as the reference's DXF driver writes it**: the reader's font decides where each word ends, and the height is the em, not the cap height.
  - **Aligned (group 72 = 3), the reference's rule exactly** (the height follows the width): a short word of narrow letters is drawn tall in a wide font.
  - **One TEXT per line or per glyph**: see Why.
  - **Poppler's word boxes** (`pdftotext -bbox`): no font, and a box from a default ascent.
  - **Refusing a page with any glyph without text**: both files set only in bitmap fonts would stay refused, for a few ligatures and symbols on each page (52 and 50 lone marks on 3,484 and 2,921 glyphs).
  - **Reading the TeX OT1 layout into the ligature codes** (12 is `fi` in a TeX text font): the PDF does not say that a Type 3 font follows that layout, so it would be a guess.
  - **Tracing the pictures into outlines**: entry 74.
- **Verified by**: `tests/dxf-bitmap-font-text.test.ts`, red before the change (15 of 15): placement, Fit alignment and STYLE table of a page built like Poppler's; the cap height from capital letters and from the tallest glyph; outline text and white space not written; a page without glyph pictures written byte for byte as before without reading its text layer; the 4/3 scale of a page Poppler 26 writes in points; the string encoding; `#` for a glyph without text and the refusal when such glyphs are the majority; not-horizontal text and a 256-character string refused; hostile pages: a mask of 30,000 pictures used 30,000 times (one TEXT each), 34,000 uses past the element budget refused before the text layer is read, 200,000 runs on one baseline, each within 5 s. `tests/postscript-real-file-refusals.test.ts`, red before the change (12 of the 30 tests that ran; the ezdxf audits were skipped on the machine without ezdxf): the three files convert, every page with TEXT in the STANDARD style, word recall and agreement at least 0.95 against `pdftotext`, ezdxf audits every page with no error; the nine others are refused for something other than the bitmap-font text and match their row of `known-refusals.json`. `tests/svg-bitmap-glyph.test.ts` (the EMF, WMF and plain SVG refusals) passes unchanged. `ORACLE_STRICT_MODE=1 npm run bench:quality -- --parity --quality-only --quick --family vector` gives the same 39 rows on the base and the head (one of them, `vector/shapes.svg->wmf/ssim`, below the reference on both, on this macOS host).
- **Revisit when**: the DXF writer writes line types, pen widths or clips (then the nine files can follow); the text reader takes the document password; a file holds rotated bitmap-font text; or a source of a Type 3 font's encoding is found that would let ligature codes be read without a guess.
